| AMY | Chiedilo ad [[characters/agatha\|Agatha]]. |  
| GERALD | Ma insomma, che c'è? Dove va? |  
| AMY | Chiedilo ad [[characters/agatha\|Agatha]]. |  
| VIOLET | Non capisco proprio nulla. Perché se ne va? |  
| AMY | Chiedilo ad [[characters/agatha\|Agatha]]. |  
| VIOLET | Davvero, a volte mi sembra<br><br><br>d'essere l'unica persona sana di mente in questa casa.<br>Il vostro contegno mi pare del tutto inspiegabile.<br>Che cosa è successo, Amy? |  
| AMY | Harry se ne va — per farsi missionario. |  
| HARRY | Ma …! |  
| CHARLES | Un missionario! non è mai accaduto nella nostra famiglia!<br><br><br>E perché con tanta fretta? Prima di decidere … |  
| VIOLET | Non puoi davvero pensare di vivere in un clima tropicale! |  
| GERALD | Non c'è nulla di male in un clima tropicale —<br><br><br>ma bisogna sottoporsi a una qualche preparazione;<br>le nozioni mediche sono la prima cosa.<br>Ho incontrato missionari, abbastanza spesso —<br>alcuni tipi molto per bene. Una professione calunniata.<br>A volte sono utilissimi, conoscendo gli indigeni,<br>benché ogni tanto fastidiosi. Ma dovrai imparare la lingua<br><br><br>e parecchi dialetti. Vuol dire molta preparazione. |  
| VIOLET | E ti occorre una qualche qualifica religiosa!<br><br><br>Credo che dovresti consultare il vicario … |  
| GERALD | E non dimenticare<br><br><br>che ti serviranno svariate inoculazioni —<br>dipende da dove vai. |  
| CHARLES | Una cosa simile<br><br><br>non è mai accaduta nella nostra famiglia. |  
| VIOLET | Non riesco a capirlo. |  
| HARRY | Non ho mai detto che sarei andato a farmi missionario.<br><br><br>Spiegherei, ma nessuno di voi ci crederebbe;<br>e se ci credeste, non capireste ugualmente.<br>Non potete sapere perché parto. Non avete visto<br>ciò che io ho visto. Oh, perché renderlo così ridicolo<br>proprio ora? Voglio soltanto, vi prego,<br>meno chiasso possibile. Dovrete abituarvici;<br>intanto chiedo scusa delle mie cattive maniere.<br>Ma se poteste capire ne sareste del tutto contenti,<br><br><br>e dunque vi dirò addio, finché non ci rivedremo. |  
| GERALD | [[motifs/well\|Ebbene]], se sei deciso, Harry, dobbiamo accettarlo;<br><br><br>ma è una brutta [[motifs/night\|notte]], e dovrai stare attento.<br>Porti Downing con te? |  
| HARRY | Oh sì, porto Downing.<br><br><br>Non devi [[concepts/fear\|temere]] ch'io corra alcun pericolo<br>di quegli incidenti che capitano ad [[characters/arthur\|Arthur]] e a John:<br>bada tu a loro. Il mio indirizzo, madre,<br><br><br>sarà presso la banca di [[settings/london\|Londra]], finché non avrai mie notizie.<br>Addio, madre. |  
| AMY | Addio, Harry. |  
| HARRY | Addio. |  
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | Addio. |  
| HARRY | Addio, Mary. |  
| MARY | Addio, Harry. Abbi cura di te. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Esce HARRY] |  
| AMY | Alla mia età comincio appena a comprendere la [[concepts/truth\|verità]]<br><br><br>sulle cose troppo tardi per rimediarvi: e questo è essere vecchi.<br>Nondimeno sono lieta se posso arrivare a conoscerle.<br>Ho sempre voluto troppo per i miei figli,<br>più di quanto la vita possa dare. E ora ne sono punita.<br>Gerald! tu sei la persona più stupida in questa stanza,<br>Violet, tu la più maligna in modo innocuo;<br>preferisco la vostra compagnia a quella di chiunque altro<br>solo per farmi accompagnare nella stanza accanto. Dove possa distendermi.<br>Poi potrete lasciarmi. |  
| GERALD | Oh, certamente, Amy. |  
| VIOLET | Non capisco<br><br><br>una sola cosa di quanto è accaduto. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Escono AMY, VIOLET, GERALD] |  
| CHARLES | È molto strano,<br><br><br>ma comincio a sentire, comincio appena a sentire<br>che c'è qualcosa che potrei capire, se me lo dicessero.<br><br><br>Ma non sono sicuro di volerlo sapere. Suppongo d'invecchiare:<br>la vecchiaia era salita dolcemente fin qui. Mi sentivo abbastanza sicuro;<br>e ora non mi sento sicuro. Come se la terra dovesse aprirsi<br>fino al centro, mentre sto per [[motifs/cross\|attraversare]] Pall Mall.<br>Credevo che la vita non potesse portare altre sorprese;<br>ma ora ricordo che sono sempre sorpreso<br>dal bulldog nella Burlington Arcade.<br>E se ogni istante fosse così, se si fosse desti?<br>Voi due pare che ne sappiate più di me. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Entra DOWNING, in fretta, in costume da autista] |  
| DOWNING | Oh, mi scusi, signorina, mi scusi, signor Charles:<br><br><br>Sua Signoria mi ha rimandato indietro perché s'è ricordato<br>che crede d'aver lasciato il portasigarette sul tavolo.<br>Oh, eccolo. Grazie. Buona [[motifs/night\|notte]], signorina; buona [[motifs/night\|notte]],<br>signorina Mary; buona [[motifs/night\|notte]], signore. |  
| MARY | Downing, mi prometti di non lasciare mai Sua Signoria<br><br><br>mentre siete via? |  
| DOWNING | Oh, certamente, signorina;<br><br><br>non lo lascerò mai finché avrà bisogno di me. |  
| MARY | Ma avrà bisogno di te. Non devi lasciarlo mai. |  
| DOWNING | Le parrà da ridere, quel che sto per dire —<br><br><br>ma non è poi così strano, signorina, a guardarci bene:<br>dopo tutti questi anni che sto con lui<br>credo di capire Sua Signoria meglio di chiunque altro;<br>e ho una specie di presentimento che Sua Signoria non avrà bisogno di me<br>ancora per molto. Non so darle ragioni.<br>Ma per mostrarle che cosa intendo, benché stenterà a crederlo,<br>ho sempre detto che qualunque cosa capitasse a Sua Signoria<br>era solo una specie di preparazione a qualcos'altro.<br>Non ho il dono della parola, ma so bene che cosa intendo:<br>i più di noi paiono vivere secondo le circostanze,<br>ma con le persone come lui c'è qualcosa dentro<br>che spiega ciò che accade loro. Se ne ha il sentore.<br>Così mi pare di sapere in anticipo, quando sta per accadere qualcosa,<br>e pare del tutto naturale, trattandosi di Sua Signoria.<br>Ed è per questo che dico ora: ho il presentimento<br>che non avrà bisogno di me a lungo, e non avrà bisogno di nessuno. |  
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | E, Downing, se il suo modo di fare ti parrà inspiegabile<br><br><br>a tratti, non devi darti pena per questo.<br>È sano di mente in tutto e per tutto quanto te o me,<br>vede il mondo con la stessa chiarezza con cui lo vediamo tu ed io,<br>solo che ha visto assai più di questo,<br>e anche noi le abbiamo vedute — la signorina Mary ed io. |  
| DOWNING | La capisco, signorina. E se posso dirlo,<br>ora che ha toccato l'argomento, sono molto sollevato —<br>se mi spiego. Pensavo fosse quella la ragione<br>per cui si partiva stasera. Anzi, quasi me l'aspettavo,<br>tant'è che avevo l'automobile già pronta. Intende quei [[motifs/ghosts\|fantasmi]], signorina!<br>Mi domandavo quando Sua Signoria sarebbe arrivato a vederli —<br>e così li ha visti anche lei! Devono averle fatto un certo effetto!<br>A me sì, sul principio. Ci si abitua presto.<br>Certo, sapevo che avevano a che fare con Sua Signoria<br>e non con me, sicché potevo guardarli allegramente,<br>per modo di dire. Non c'è male in loro,<br>ci giurerei. Sarà tutto, signorina? |  
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | Sarà tutto, grazie, Downing. Non dobbiamo trattenerti;<br><br><br>Sua Signoria si starà chiedendo perché tardi tanto. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Esce DOWNING. Entra IVY] |  
| IVY | Dove va Downing? dov'è Harry?<br><br><br>Guardate. È arrivato un telegramma da [[characters/arthur\|Arthur]]; |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Entrano GERALD e VIOLET] |  
| IVY | Mi [[concepts/wonder\|domando]] perché l'abbia mandato, dopo aver telefonato.<br>Ve lo leggo? Mi chiedevo<br>se mostrarlo o no ad Amy. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Legge] |  
| IVY | «Spiacente ritardo affari in città molti auguri ci vediamo domani molti auguri urrà con [[motifs/love\|affetto]] [[characters/arthur\|Arthur]]».<br><br><br>Voglio dire, dopo ciò che sappiamo di quanto è realmente accaduto,<br>credete che Amy debba vederlo? |  
| VIOLET | No, certamente no.<br><br><br>Tu non sai ciò che è successo, Ivy.<br>E se lo sapessi, non lo capiresti.<br>Io non capisco, dunque come potresti tu? Amy non sta [[motifs/well\|bene]];<br>e sta riposando. |  
| IVY | Oh, mi dispiace. Ma non potete spiegare?<br><br><br>Perché avete tutti quell'aria strana? Credo che mi si possa permettere<br>di sapere che cosa è accaduto.<br>VOCE DI AMY.                             [[characters/agatha\|Agatha]]! Mary! venite!<br><br><br>L'[[motifs/clock\|orologio]] s'è fermato nel buio! |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Escono [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] e MARY. Pausa. Entra WARBURTON] |  
| WARBURTON | [[motifs/well\|Ebbene]]! è una [[motifs/night\|notte]] schifosa per stare fuori.<br><br><br>Ecco perché ho tardato tanto, ad andare e a tornare.<br>Ma sono lieto di dire che John si rimette bene;<br>non era grave come faceva credere Winchell,<br>e domattina lo avremo quassù.<br>[[concepts/hope\|Spero]] che Lady Monchensey non si sia data pena?<br>Ho fretta di tranquillizzarla. Ma che c'è, che cosa succede? |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Entra MARY] |  
| MARY | Dottor Warburton! |  
| WARBURTON | Scusatemi. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Escono MARY e WARBURTON] |  
| [[characters/chorus\|CORO]] | Non ci piace guardare dalla stessa [[motifs/window\|finestra]] e vedere un paesaggio tutto diverso.<br><br><br>Non ci piace salire una scala e scoprire che ci porta in basso.<br>Non ci piace uscire da una porta e ritrovarci nella stessa stanza.<br><br><br>Non ci piace il labirinto nel [[motifs/garden\|giardino]], perché troppo somiglia al labirinto nel cervello.<br><br><br>Non ci piace ciò che accade da svegli, perché troppo somiglia a ciò che accade nel sonno.<br><br><br>Comprendiamo gli affari ordinari del vivere,<br>sappiamo far funzionare la [[motifs/machine\|macchina]],<br>di solito sappiamo evitare gli incidenti,<br>siamo assicurati contro l'[[motifs/fire\|incendio]],<br>contro il furto e la malattia,<br>contro gli impianti difettosi,<br>ma non contro l'atto di Dio.<br>Conosciamo vari sortilegi e incantesimi,<br>e forme minori di stregoneria,<br>divinazione e chiromanzia,<br>specifici contro l'insonnia,<br>la lombaggine e la perdita di denaro.<br>Ma il cerchio della nostra comprensione<br>è un'area assai ristretta.<br>Salvo che per un numero limitato<br>di scopi strettamente pratici<br>non sappiamo ciò che facciamo;<br>e perfino, a pensarci bene,<br>non sappiamo molto del pensare.<br>Che cosa accade fuori del cerchio?<br>E qual è il senso dell'accadere?<br>Quale agguato sta oltre l'erica<br>e dietro le Pietre Erette?<br>Oltre lo strato di Heaviside<br>e dietro la [[motifs/moon\|luna]] sorridente?<br>E che cosa si fa di noi?<br>E che cosa siamo, e che cosa facciamo?<br>A ciascuna e a tutte queste domande<br>non v'è risposta concepibile.<br>Abbiamo sofferto assai più d'una perdita personale —<br>abbiamo smarrito la via nel buio. |  
| IVY | Dovrò restare fino a dopo il funerale: il mio biglietto per [[settings/london\|Londra]] sarà ancora valido? |  
| GERALD | Non attendo con piacere di dover trattare con [[characters/arthur\|Arthur]] e John domattina. |  
| VIOLET | Dobbiamo aspettare che si legga il testamento. Manderò un telegramma domattina. |  
| CHARLES | [[concepts/fear\|Temo]] che la mia mente non sia più quella che era — o lo era? — eppure credo che potrei capire. |  
| TUTTI | Ma dobbiamo adattarci al momento: dobbiamo fare la cosa giusta. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Escono] |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Entrano, da una porta, [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] e MARY, e collocano un tavolinetto portatile. Da un'altra porta entra DENMAN recando una torta di compleanno con le candeline accese, che posa sul tavolo. Esce DENMAN. [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] e MARY camminano lentamente in fila indiana intorno e intorno al tavolo, in senso [[motifs/clock\|orario]]. A ogni giro spengono qualche candelina, sicché le loro ultime parole sono dette al buio.] |  
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | Una maledizione è lenta a venire<br><br><br>a piena maturazione:<br>non si può affrettare<br>e non si può ritardare |  
| MARY | Non si può deviare:<br><br><br>un tentativo di deviarla<br>non fa che implicare altri<br>nel giorno del compimento |  
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | Una maledizione è una potenza<br><br><br>non soggetta a ragione:<br>ogni maledizione ha il suo corso,<br>il suo modo d'espiazione<br>                    Segui segui |  
| MARY | Non nel diurno [[motifs/time\|tempo]]<br><br><br>e nel mondo di qua<br>dove sappiamo ciò che facciamo<br>non è la sua operazione<br>                    Segui segui |  
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | Ma nel [[motifs/night\|notturno]] [[motifs/time\|tempo]]<br><br><br>e nel mondo di sotto<br>dove le maglie che abbiamo tessute<br>ci legano l'uno all'altro<br>                    Segui segui |  
| MARY | Una maledizione è scritta<br><br><br>sul rovescio delle cose<br>dietro lo [[motifs/mirror\|specchio]] sorridente<br>e dietro la [[motifs/moon\|luna]] sorridente<br>                    Segui segui |  
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | Per questa via il pellegrinaggio<br><br><br>d'espiazione<br>intorno e intorno al cerchio<br>a compiere l'incanto<br>sì che il nodo sia snodato<br>l'incrociato disincrociato<br>il torto raddrizzato<br>e la maledizione finita<br>per intercessione<br>per pellegrinaggio<br>per opera di quanti partono<br>in più direzioni<br>per la propria redenzione<br>e per quella dei defunti —<br>                                                Possano riposare in pace.<br><br>IL COCKTAIL PARTY<br>Personaggi<br>[[characters/edward-chamberlayne\|EDWARD CHAMBERLAYNE]]<br>[[characters/julia\|JULIA]] (SIGNORA SHUTTLETHWAITE)<br><br><br>[[characters/celia-coplestone\|CELIA COPLESTONE]]<br>ALEXANDER MACCOLGIE GIBBS<br>PETER QUILPE<br>UN OSPITE NON IDENTIFICATO, poi identificato come [[characters/sir-henry-harcourt-reilly\|SIR HENRY HARCOURT-REILLY]]<br>[[characters/lavinia-chamberlayne\|LAVINIA CHAMBERLAYNE]]<br>UNA [[characters/nurse\|INFERMIERA]]-SEGRETARIA<br>DUE UOMINI DEL SERVIZIO DI RISTORO<br>La scena è a [[settings/london\|Londra]] |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_1" data-title="The Family Reunion — Part I, Scene 1" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/shadow,motif/clock,motif/dance,motif/death,motif/desert,motif/fire,motif/garden,motif/ghosts,motif/home,motif/moon,motif/night,motif/nightingale,motif/sleep,motif/spring,motif/storm,motif/swallow,motif/time,motif/water,motif/well,motif/wheel,motif/wind,motif/window,motif/winter,concept/body,concept/conscience,concept/courage,concept/doubt,concept/freedom,concept/hope,concept/self,concept/solitude,concept/suffering,concept/suicide,concept/truth,concept/vanity,form/fragment,setting/desert,setting/italy,setting/london,setting/new_york,setting/paris,character/agatha,character/arthur,character/chorus"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | The drawing-room, after tea. An afternoon in late March. |
| *(didascalia)* | AMY, IVY, VIOLET, [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]], GERALD, CHARLES, MARY |
| *(didascalia)* | [DENMAN enters to draw the curtains] |
| AMY | Not yet! I will ring for you. It is still quite light.<br><br><br>I have nothing to do but watch the days draw out,<br>Now that I sit in the house from October to June,<br>And the [[motifs/swallow\|swallow]] comes too soon and the [[motifs/spring\|spring]] will be over<br>And the cuckoo will be gone before I am out again.<br>O Sun, that was once so warm, O Light that was taken for granted<br>When I was young and strong, and sun and light unsought for<br>And the [[motifs/night\|night]] unfeared and the day expected<br>And clocks could be trusted, tomorrow assured<br>And [[motifs/time\|time]] would not stop in the dark!<br>Put on the lights. But leave the curtains undrawn.<br>Make up the [[motifs/fire\|fire]]. Will the [[motifs/spring\|spring]] never come? I am cold. |
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | Wishwood was always a cold place, Amy. |
| IVY | I have always told Amy she should go south in the [[motifs/winter\|winter]].<br><br><br>Were I in Amy’s position, I would go south in the [[motifs/winter\|winter]].<br>I would follow the sun, not wait for the sun to come here.<br>I would go south in the [[motifs/winter\|winter]], if I could afford it,<br>Not freeze, as I do, in Bayswater, by a gas-[[motifs/fire\|fire]] counting shillings. |
| VIOLET | Go south! to the English circulating libraries,<br><br><br>To the military widows and the English chaplains,<br>To the chilly deck-chair and the strong cold tea —<br>The strong cold stewed bad Indian tea. |
| CHARLES | That’s not Amy’s style at all. We are country-bred people.<br><br><br>Amy has been too long used to our ways<br>Living with horses and dogs and guns<br>Ever to want to leave England in the [[motifs/winter\|winter]].<br>But a single man like me is better off in [[settings/london\|London]]:<br>A man can be very cosy at his club<br>Even in an English [[motifs/winter\|winter]]. |
| GERALD | [[motifs/well\|Well]], as for me,<br><br><br>I’d just as soon be a subaltern again<br>To be back in the East. An incomparable climate<br>For a man who can exercise a little common prudence;<br>And your servants look after you very much better. |
| AMY | My servants are perfectly competent, Gerald.<br><br><br>I can still see to that. |
| VIOLET | [[motifs/well\|Well]], as for me,<br><br><br>I would never go south, no, definitely never,<br>Even could I do it as [[motifs/well\|well]] as Amy:<br>England’s bad enough, I would never go south,<br>Simply to see the vulgarest people —<br>You can keep out of their way at [[motifs/home\|home]];<br>People with money from heaven knows where — |
| GERALD | Dividends from aeroplane shares. |
| VIOLET | They bathe all day and they [[motifs/dance\|dance]] all [[motifs/night\|night]]<br><br><br>In the absolute minimum of clothes. |
| CHARLES | It’s the cocktail-drinking does the harm:<br><br><br>There’s nothing on earth so bad for the young.<br>All that a civilised person needs<br>Is a glass of dry sherry or two before dinner.<br>The modern young people don’t know what they’re drinking,<br>Modern young people don’t care what they’re eating;<br>They’ve lost their sense of taste and smell<br>Because of their cocktails and cigarettes. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Enter DENMAN with sherry and whisky. CHARLES takes sherry and GERALD whisky.] |
| CHARLES | That’s what it comes to. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Lights a cigarette] |
| IVY | The younger generation<br><br><br>Are undoubtedly decadent. |
| CHARLES | The younger generation<br><br><br>Are not what we were. Haven’t the stamina,<br>Haven’t the sense of responsibility. |
| GERALD | You’re being very hard on the younger generation.<br><br><br>I don’t come across them very much now, myself;<br>But I must say I’ve met some very decent specimens<br>And some first-class shots — better than you were,<br>Charles, as I remember. Besides, you’ve got to make allowances:<br>We haven’t left them such an easy world to live in.<br>Let the younger generation speak for itself:<br>It’s Mary’s generation. What does she think about it? |
| MARY | Really, Cousin Gerald, if you want information<br><br><br>About the younger generation, you must ask someone else.<br>I’m afraid that I don’t deserve the compliment:<br>I don’t belong to any generation. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exit] |
| VIOLET | Really, Gerald, I must say you’re very tactless,<br><br><br>And I think that Charles might have been more considerate. |
| GERALD | I’m very sorry: but why was she upset?<br><br><br>I only meant to draw her into the conversation. |
| CHARLES | She’s a nice girl; but it’s a difficult age for her.<br><br><br>I suppose she must be getting on for thirty?<br>She ought to be married, that’s what it is. |
| AMY | So she should have been, if things had gone as I intended.<br><br><br>Harry’s return does not make things easy for her<br>At the moment: but life may still go right.<br>Meanwhile, let us drop the subject. The less said the better. |
| GERALD | That reminds me, Amy,<br><br><br>When are the boys all due to arrive? |
| AMY | I do not want the [[motifs/clock\|clock]] to stop in the dark.<br><br><br>If you want to know why I never leave Wishwood<br>That is the reason. I keep Wishwood alive<br>To keep the family alive, to keep them together,<br>To keep me alive, and I live to keep them.<br>You none of you understand how old you are<br>And [[motifs/death\|death]] will come to you as a mild surprise,<br>A momentary shudder in a vacant room.<br>Only [[characters/agatha\|Agatha]] seems to discover some meaning in [[motifs/death\|death]]<br>Which I cannot find.<br>— I am only certain of [[characters/arthur\|Arthur]] and John,<br>[[characters/arthur\|Arthur]] in [[settings/london\|London]], John in Leicestershire:<br><br><br>They should both be here in good [[motifs/time\|time]] for dinner.<br>Harry telephoned to me from Marseilles,<br>He would come by air to [[settings/paris\|Paris]], and so to [[settings/london\|London]],<br>And hoped to arrive in the course of the evening. |
| VIOLET | Harry was always the most likely to be late. |
| AMY | This [[motifs/time\|time]], it will not be his fault.<br><br><br>We are very lucky to have Harry at all. |
| IVY | And when will you have your birthday cake, Amy,<br><br><br>And open your presents? |
| AMY | After dinner:<br><br><br>That is the best [[motifs/time\|time]]. |
| IVY | It is the first [[motifs/time\|time]]<br><br><br>You have not had your cake and your presents at tea. |
| AMY | This is a very particular occasion<br><br><br>As you ought to know. It will be the first [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>For eight years that we have all been together. |
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | It is going to be rather painful for Harry<br><br><br>After eight years and all that has happened<br>To come back to Wishwood. |
| GERALD | Why, painful? |
| VIOLET | Gerald! you know what [[characters/agatha\|Agatha]] means. |
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | I mean painful, because everything is irrevocable,<br><br><br>Because the past is irremediable,<br>Because the future can only be built<br>Upon the real past. Wandering in the tropics<br>Or against the painted scene of the Mediterranean,<br>Harry must often have remembered Wishwood —<br>The nursery tea, the school holiday,<br>The daring feats on the old pony,<br>And thought to creep back through the little door.<br>He will find a new Wishwood. Adaptation is hard. |
| AMY | Nothing is changed, [[characters/agatha\|Agatha]], at Wishwood.<br><br><br>Everything is kept as it was when he left it,<br>Except the old pony, and the mongrel setter<br>Which I had to have destroyed.<br>Nothing has been changed. I have seen to that. |
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | Yes. I mean that at Wishwood he will find another Harry.<br><br><br>The man who returns will have to meet<br>The boy who left. Round by the stables,<br>In the coach-house, in the orchard,<br>In the plantation, down the corridor<br>That led to the nursery, round the corner<br>Of the new wing, he will have to face him —<br>And it will not be a very jolly corner.<br>When the loop in [[motifs/time\|time]] comes — and it does not come for everybody —<br><br><br>The hidden is revealed, and the spectres show themselves. |
| GERALD | I don’t in the least know what you’re talking about.<br><br><br>You seem to be wanting to give us all the hump.<br>I must say, this isn’t cheerful for Amy’s birthday<br>Or for Harry’s homecoming. Make him feel at [[motifs/home\|home]], I say!<br>Make him feel that what has happened doesn’t matter.<br>He’s taken his medicine, I’ve no [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]].<br>Let him marry again and carry on at Wishwood. |
| AMY | Thank you, Gerald. Though [[characters/agatha\|Agatha]] means<br><br><br>As a rule, a good deal more than she cares to betray,<br>I am bound to say that I agree with you. |
| CHARLES | I never wrote to him when he lost his wife —<br><br><br>That was just about a year ago, wasn’t it?<br>Do you think that I ought to mention it now?<br>It seems to me too late. |
| AMY | Much too late.<br><br><br>If he wants to talk about it, that’s another matter;<br>But I don’t believe he will. He will wish to forget it.<br>I do not mince matters in front of the family:<br>You can call it nothing but a blessed relief.<br>VIOLET, I call it providential. |
| IVY | Yet it must have been shocking,<br><br><br>Especially to lose anybody in that way —<br><br><br>Swept off the deck in the middle of a [[motifs/storm\|storm]],<br>And never even to recover the [[concepts/body\|body]]. |
| CHARLES | ‘[[motifs/well\|Well]]-known Peeress Vanishes from Liner’. |
| GERALD | Yes, it’s odd to think of her as permanently missing. |
| VIOLET | Had she been drinking? |
| AMY | I would never ask him. |
| IVY | These things are much better not enquired into.<br><br><br>She may have done it in a fit of temper. |
| GERALD | I never met her. |
| AMY | I am very glad you did not.<br><br><br>I am very glad that none of you ever met her.<br>It will make the situation very much easier<br>And is why I was so anxious you should all be here.<br>She never would have been one of the family,<br>She never wished to be one of the family,<br>She only wanted to keep him to herself<br>To satisfy her [[concepts/vanity\|vanity]]. That’s why she dragged him<br>All over Europe and half round the world<br>To expensive hotels and undesirable society<br>Which she could choose herself. She never wanted<br>Harry’s relations or Harry’s old friends;<br>She never wanted to fit herself to Harry,<br>But only to bring Harry down to her own level.<br>A restless shivering painted [[archetypes/shadow\|shadow]]<br>In life, she is less than a [[archetypes/shadow\|shadow]] in [[motifs/death\|death]].<br>You might as [[motifs/well\|well]] all of you know the [[concepts/truth\|truth]]<br>For the sake of the future. There can be no grief<br>And no regret and no remorse.<br>I would have prevented it if I could. For the sake of the future:<br>Harry is to take command at Wishwood<br>And I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] we can contrive his future happiness.<br>Do not discuss his absence. Please behave only<br>As if nothing had happened in the last eight years. |
| GERALD | That will be a little difficult. |
| VIOLET | Nonsense, Gerald!<br><br><br>You must see for yourself it’s the only thing to do. |
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | Thus with most careful devotion<br><br><br>Thus with precise attention<br>To detail, interfering preparation<br>Of that which is already prepared<br>Men tighten the knot of confusion<br>Into perfect misunderstanding,<br>Reflecting a pocket-torch of observation<br>Upon each other’s opacity<br>Neglecting all the admonitions<br>From the world around the corner<br>The [[motifs/wind\|wind]]’s talk in the dry holly-tree<br>The inclination of the [[motifs/moon\|moon]]<br>The attraction of the dark passage<br>The paw under the door.<br>[[characters/chorus\|CHORUS]] (IVY, VIOLET, GERALD and CHARLES). Why do we feel embarrassed, impatient, fretful, ill at ease,<br><br><br>Assembled like amateur actors who have not been assigned their parts?<br><br><br>Like amateur actors in a dream when the curtain rises, to find themselves dressed for a different play, or having rehearsed the wrong parts,<br><br><br>Waiting for the rustling in the stalls, the titter in the dress circle, the laughter and catcalls in the gallery? |
| CHARLES | I might have been in St. James’s Street, in a comfortable chair rather nearer the [[motifs/fire\|fire]]. |
| IVY | I might have been visiting Cousin Lily at Sidmouth, if I had not had to come to this party. |
| GERALD | I might have been staying with Compton-Smith, down at his place in Dorset. |
| VIOLET | I should have been helping Lady Bumpus, at the Vicar’s American Tea. |
| [[characters/chorus\|CHORUS]] | Yet we are here at Amy’s command, to play an unread part in some monstrous farce, ridiculous in some nightmare pantomime. |
| AMY | What’s that? I thought I saw someone pass the [[motifs/window\|window]].<br><br><br>What [[motifs/time\|time]] is it? |
| CHARLES | Nearly twenty to seven. |
| AMY | John should be here now, he has the shortest way to come.<br><br><br>John at least, if not [[characters/arthur\|Arthur]]. Hark, there is someone coming:<br>Yes, it must be John. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Enter HARRY] |
| AMY |                                      Harry! |
| *(didascalia)* | [HARRY stops suddenly at the door and stares at the [[motifs/window\|window]]] |
| IVY | Welcome, Harry! |
| GERALD | [[motifs/well\|Well]] done! |
| VIOLET | Welcome [[motifs/home\|home]] to Wishwood! |
| CHARLES | Why, what’s the matter? |
| AMY | Harry, if you want the curtains drawn you should let me ring for Denman. |
| HARRY | How can you sit in this blaze of light for all the world to look at?<br><br><br>If you knew how you looked, when I saw you through the [[motifs/window\|window]]!<br>Do you like to be stared at by eyes through a [[motifs/window\|window]]? |
| AMY | You forget, Harry, that you are at Wishwood,<br><br><br>Not in town, where you have to close the blinds.<br>There is no one to see you but our servants who belong here.<br>And who all want to see you back, Harry. |
| HARRY | Look there, look there: do you see them? |
| GERALD | No, I don’t see anyone about. |
| HARRY | No, no, not there. Look there!<br><br><br>Can’t you see them? You don’t see them, but I see them,<br><br><br>And they see me. This is the first [[motifs/time\|time]] that I have seen them.<br>In the Java Straits, in the Sunda Sea,<br>In the sweet sickly tropical [[motifs/night\|night]], I knew they were coming.<br>In [[settings/italy\|Italy]], from behind the [[motifs/nightingale\|nightingale]]’s thicket,<br>The eyes stared at me, and corrupted that song.<br>Behind the palm trees in the Grand Hotel<br>They were always there. But I did not see them.<br><br><br>Why should they wait until I came back to Wishwood?<br>There were a thousand places where I might have met them!<br>Why here? why here?<br>                                     Many happy returns of the day, mother.<br>Aunt Ivy, Aunt Violet, Uncle Gerald, Uncle Charles. [[characters/agatha\|Agatha]]. |
| AMY | We are very glad to have you back, Harry.<br><br><br>Now we shall all be together for dinner.<br>The servants have been looking forward to your coming:<br>Would you like to have them in after dinner<br>Or wait till tomorrow? I am sure you must be tired.<br>You will find everybody here, and everything the same.<br>Mr. Bevan — you remember — wants to call tomorrow<br>On some legal business, a question about taxes —<br>But I think you would rather wait till you are rested.<br>Your room is all ready for you. Nothing has been changed. |
| HARRY | Changed? nothing changed? how can you say that nothing is changed?<br><br><br>You all look so withered and young. |
| GERALD | We must have a ride tomorrow.<br><br><br>You’ll find you know the country as [[motifs/well\|well]] as ever.<br>There wasn’t an inch of it you didn’t know.<br>But you’ll have to see about a couple of new hunters. |
| CHARLES | And I’ve a new wine merchant to recommend you;<br><br><br>Your cellar could do with a little attention. |
| IVY | And you’ll really have to find a successor to old Hawkins.<br><br><br>It’s really high [[motifs/time\|time]] the old man was pensioned.<br>He’s let the rock [[motifs/garden\|garden]] go to rack and ruin,<br>And he’s nearly half blind. I’ve spoken to your mother<br>[[motifs/time\|Time]] and [[motifs/time\|time]] again: she’s done nothing about it<br>Because she preferred to wait for your coming. |
| VIOLET | And [[motifs/time\|time]] and [[motifs/time\|time]] again I have spoken to your mother<br>About the waste that goes on in the kitchen.<br>Mrs. Packell is too old to know what she is doing.<br>It really needs a man in charge of things at Wishwood. |
| AMY | You see your aunts and uncles are very helpful, Harry.<br><br><br>I have always found them forthcoming with advice<br>Which I have never taken. Now it is your business.<br>I have only struggled to keep Wishwood going<br>And to make no changes before your return.<br>Now it’s for you to manage. I am an old woman.<br>They can give me no further advice when I’m dead. |
| IVY | Oh, dear Amy!<br><br><br>No one wants you to die, I’m sure!<br>Now that Harry’s back, is the [[motifs/time\|time]] to think of living. |
| HARRY | [[motifs/time\|Time]] and [[motifs/time\|time]] and [[motifs/time\|time]], and change, no change!<br><br><br>You all of you try to talk as if nothing had happened,<br>And yet you are talking of nothing else. Why not get to the point<br>Or if you want to pretend that I am another person —<br>A person that you have conspired to invent, please do so<br>In my absence. I shall be less embarrassing to you. [[characters/agatha\|Agatha]]? |
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | I think, Harry, that having got so far —<br><br><br>If you want no pretences, let us have no pretences:<br>And you must try at once to make us understand,<br>And we must try to understand you. |
| HARRY | But how can I explain, how can I explain to you?<br><br><br>You will understand less after I have explained it.<br>All that I could [[concepts/hope\|hope]] to make you understand<br>Is only events: not what has happened.<br>And people to whom nothing has ever happened<br>Cannot understand the unimportance of events. |
| GERALD | [[motifs/well\|Well]], you can’t say that nothing has happened to me.<br><br><br>I started as a youngster on the North-West Frontier —<br>Been in tight corners most of my life<br>And some pretty nasty messes. |
| CHARLES | And there isn’t much would surprise me, Harry;<br><br><br>Or shock me, either. |
| HARRY | You are all people<br><br><br>To whom nothing has happened, at most a continual impact<br>Of external events. You have gone through life in [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]],<br>Never woken to the nightmare. I tell you, life would be unendurable<br>If you were wide awake. You do not know<br>The noxious smell untraceable in the drains,<br>Inaccessible to the plumbers, that has its hour of the [[motifs/night\|night]]; you do not know<br><br><br>The unspoken voice of sorrow in the ancient bedroom<br>At three o’[[motifs/clock\|clock]] in the morning. I am not speaking<br>Of my own experience, but trying to give you<br>Comparisons in a more familiar medium. I am the old house<br>With the noxious smell and the sorrow before morning,<br>In which all past is present, all degradation<br>Is unredeemable. As for what happens —<br>Of the past you can only see what is past,<br>Not what is always present. That is what matters. |
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | Nevertheless, Harry, best tell us as you can:<br><br><br>Talk in your own language, without stopping to debate<br>Whether it may be too far beyond our understanding. |
| HARRY | The sudden [[concepts/solitude\|solitude]] in a crowded [[motifs/desert\|desert]]<br><br><br>In a thick smoke, many creatures moving<br>Without direction, for no direction<br>Leads anywhere but round and round in that vapour —<br>Without purpose, and without principle of conduct<br>In flickering intervals of light and darkness;<br>The partial anæsthesia of [[concepts/suffering\|suffering]] without feeling<br>And partial observation of one’s own automatism<br>While the slow stain sinks deeper through the skin<br>Tainting the flesh and discolouring the bone —<br>This is what matters, but it is unspeakable,<br>Untranslatable: I talk in general terms<br>Because the particular has no language. One thinks to escape<br>By violence, but one is still alone<br>In an over-crowded [[motifs/desert\|desert]], jostled by [[motifs/ghosts\|ghosts]].<br>It was only reversing the senseless direction<br>For a momentary rest on the burning [[motifs/wheel\|wheel]]<br>That cloudless [[motifs/night\|night]] in the mid-Atlantic<br>When I pushed her over. |
| VIOLET | Pushed her? |
| HARRY | You would never imagine anyone could sink so quickly.<br><br><br>I had always supposed, wherever I went<br>That she would be with me; whatever I did<br>That she was unkillable. It was not like that.<br>Everything is true in a different sense.<br>I expected to find her when I went back to the cabin.<br>Later, I became excited, I think I made enquiries;<br>The purser and the steward were extremely sympathetic<br>And the doctor very attentive.<br>That [[motifs/night\|night]] I slept heavily, alone. |
| AMY | Harry! |
| CHARLES | You mustn’t indulge such dangerous fancies.<br><br><br>It’s only doing harm to your mother and yourself.<br>Of course we know what really happened, we read it in the papers —<br>No need to revert to it. Remember, my boy,<br>I understand, your life together made it seem more horrible.<br>There’s a lot in my own past life that presses on my chest<br>When I wake, as I do now, early before morning.<br>I understand these feelings better than you know —<br>But you have no reason to reproach yourself.<br><br><br>Your [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]] can be clear. |
| HARRY | It goes a good deal deeper<br><br><br>Than what people call their [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]]; it is just the cancer<br>That eats away the [[concepts/self\|self]]. I knew how you would take it.<br>First of all, you isolate the single event<br>As something so dreadful that it couldn’t have happened,<br>Because you could not bear it. So you must believe<br>That I suffer from delusions. It is not my [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]],<br>Not my mind, that is diseased, but the world I have to live in.<br>— I lay two days in contented drowsiness;<br>Then I recovered. I am afraid of [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]:<br>A condition in which one can be caught for the last [[motifs/time\|time]].<br>And also waking. She is nearer than ever.<br>The contamination has reached the marrow<br>And they are always near. Here, nearer than ever.<br><br><br>They are very close here. I had not expected that. |
| AMY | Harry, Harry, you are very tired<br><br><br>And overwrought. Coming so far<br>And making such haste, the change is too sudden for you.<br>You are unused to our foggy climate<br>And the northern country. When you see Wishwood<br>Again by day, all will be the same again.<br>I beg you to go now and rest before dinner.<br>Get Downing to draw you a hot bath,<br>And you will feel better. |
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | There are certain points I do not yet understand:<br><br><br>They will be clear later. I am also convinced<br>That you only hold a [[forms/fragment\|fragment]] of the explanation.<br>It is only because of what you do not understand<br>That you feel the need to declare what you do.<br>There is more to understand: hold fast to that<br>As the way to [[concepts/freedom\|freedom]]. |
| HARRY | I think I see what you mean,<br><br><br>Dimly — as you once explained the sobbing in the chimney<br>The evil in the dark closet, which they said was not there,<br>Which they explained away, but you explained them<br>Or at least, made me cease to be afraid of them.<br>I will go and have my bath. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exit] |
| GERALD | God preserve us!<br><br><br>I never thought it would be as bad as this. |
| VIOLET | There is only one thing to be done:<br><br><br>Harry must see a doctor. |
| IVY | But I understand —<br><br><br>I have heard of such cases before — that people in his condition<br>Often betray the most immoderate resentment<br>At such a suggestion. They can be very cunning —<br>Their malady makes them so. They do not want to be cured<br>And they know what you are thinking. |
| CHARLES | He has probably let this notion grow in his mind,<br><br><br>Living among strangers, with no one to talk to.<br>I suspect it is simply that the wish to get rid of her<br>Makes him believe he did. He cannot trust his good fortune.<br>I believe that all he needs is someone to talk to,<br>To get it off his mind. I’ll have a talk to him tomorrow. |
| AMY | Most certainly not, Charles, you are not the right person.<br><br><br>I prefer to believe that a few days at Wishwood<br>Among his own family, is all that he needs. |
| GERALD | Nevertheless, Amy, there’s something in Violet’s suggestion.<br><br><br>Why not ring up Warburton, and ask him to join us?<br>He’s an old friend of the family, it’s perfectly natural<br>That he should be asked. He looked after all the boys<br>When they were children. I’ll have a word with him.<br>He can talk to Harry, and Harry need have no suspicion.<br>I’d trust Warburton’s opinion. |
| AMY | If anyone speaks to Dr. Warburton<br><br><br>It should be myself. What does [[characters/agatha\|Agatha]] think? |
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | It seems a necessary move<br><br><br>In an unnecessary action,<br>Not for the good that it will do<br>But that nothing may be left undone<br>On the margin of the impossible. |
| AMY | Very [[motifs/well\|well]].<br><br><br>I will ring up the doctor myself. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exit] |
| CHARLES | Meanwhile, I have an idea. Why not question Downing?<br><br><br>He’s been with Harry ten years, he’s absolutely discreet.<br>He was with them on the boat. He might be of use. |
| IVY | Charles! you don’t really suppose<br><br><br>That he might have pushed her over? |
| CHARLES | In any case, I shouldn’t blame Harry.<br><br><br>I might have done the same thing once, myself.<br>Nobody knows what he’s likely to do<br>Until there’s somebody he wants to get rid of. |
| GERALD | Even so, we don’t want Downing to know<br><br><br>Any more than he knows already.<br>And even if he knew, it’s very much better<br>That he shouldn’t know that we knew it also.<br>Why not let sleeping dogs lie? |
| CHARLES | All the same, there’s a question or two |
| *(didascalia)* | [Rings the bell] |
| CHARLES | That I’d like to ask Downing.<br>                                                 He shan’t know why I’m asking. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Enter DENMAN] |
| CHARLES | Denman, where is Downing? Is he up with his Lordship? |
| DENMAN | He’s out in the garage, Sir, with his Lordship’s car. |
| CHARLES | Tell him I’d like to have a word with him, please. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exit DENMAN] |
| VIOLET | Charles, if you are determined upon this investigation.<br><br><br>Which I am convinced is going to lead us nowhere,<br>And which I am sure Amy would disapprove of —<br>I only wish to express my emphatic protest<br>Both against your purpose and the means you are employing. |
| CHARLES | My purpose is, to find out what’s wrong with Harry:<br><br><br>Until we know that, we can do nothing for him.<br>And as for my means, we can’t afford to be squeamish<br>In taking hold of anything that comes to hand.<br>If you are interested in helping Harry<br>You can hardly object to the means. |
| VIOLET | I do object. |
| IVY | And I wish to associate myself with my sister<br><br><br>In her objections — |
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | I have no objection,<br><br><br>Any more than I object to asking Dr. Warburton:<br>I only see that this is all quite irrelevant;<br>We had better leave Charles to talk to Downing<br>And pursue his own methods. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Rises] |
| VIOLET | I do not agree.<br><br><br>I think there should be witnesses. I intend to remain.<br>And I wish to be present to hear what Downing says.<br>I want to know at once, not be told about it later. |
| IVY | And I shall stay with Violet. |
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | I shall return<br><br><br>When Downing has left you. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exit] |
| CHARLES | [[motifs/well\|Well]], I’m very sorry<br><br><br>You all see it like this: but there simply are times<br>When there’s nothing to do but take the bull by the horns,<br>And this is one. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Knock: and enter DOWNING] |
| CHARLES | Good evening, Downing.<br><br><br>It’s good to see you again, after all these years.<br>You’re [[motifs/well\|well]], I [[concepts/hope\|hope]]? |
| DOWNING | Thank you, very [[motifs/well\|well]] indeed, Sir. |
| CHARLES | I’m sorry to send for you so abruptly,<br><br><br>But I’ve a question I’d like to put to you,<br>I’m sure you won’t mind, it’s about his Lordship.<br>You’ve looked after his Lordship for over ten years … |
| DOWNING | Eleven years, Sir, next Lady Day. |
| CHARLES | Eleven years, and you know him pretty [[motifs/well\|well]].<br><br><br>And I’m sure that you’ve been a good friend to him, too.<br>We haven’t seen him for nearly eight years;<br><br><br>And to tell the [[concepts/truth\|truth]], now that we’ve seen him,<br>We’re a little worried about his health.<br>He doesn’t seem to be … quite himself. |
| DOWNING | Quite natural, if I may say so, Sir,<br><br><br>After what happened. |
| CHARLES | Quite so, quite.<br><br><br>Downing, you were with them on the voyage from [[settings/new-york\|New York]] —<br>We didn’t learn very much about the circumstances;<br>We only knew what we read in the papers —<br>Of course, there was a great deal too much in the papers.<br>Downing, do you think that it might have been [[concepts/suicide\|suicide]],<br>And that his Lordship knew it? |
| DOWNING | Unlikely, Sir, if I may say so.<br><br><br>Much more likely to have been an accident.<br>I mean, knowing her Ladyship,<br>I don’t think she had the [[concepts/courage\|courage]]. |
| CHARLES | Did she ever talk of [[concepts/suicide\|suicide]]? |
| DOWNING | Oh yes, she did, every now and again.<br><br><br>But in my opinion, it is those that talk<br>That are the least likely. To my way of thinking<br>She only did it to frighten people.<br>If you take my meaning — just for the effect. |
| CHARLES | I understand, Downing. Was she in good spirits? |
| DOWNING | [[motifs/well\|Well]], always about the same, Sir.<br><br><br>What I mean is, always up and down.<br>Down in the morning, and up in the evening,<br>And then she used to get rather excited,<br><br><br>And, in a way, irresponsible, Sir.<br>If I may make so bold, Sir,<br>I always thought that a very few cocktails<br>Went a long way with her Ladyship.<br>She wasn’t one of those that are designed for drinking:<br><br><br>It’s natural for some and unnatural for others. |
| CHARLES | And how was his Lordship, during the voyage? |
| DOWNING | [[motifs/well\|Well]], you might say depressed, Sir.<br><br><br>But you know his Lordship was always very quiet:<br>Very uncommon that I saw him in high spirits.<br>For what my judgment’s worth, I always said his Lordship<br>Suffered from what they call a kind of repression.<br>But what struck me … more nervous than usual;<br>I mean to say, you could see that he was nervous.<br>He behaved as if he thought something might happen. |
| CHARLES | What sort of thing? |
| DOWNING | [[motifs/well\|Well]], I don’t know, Sir.<br><br><br>But he seemed very anxious about my Lady.<br>Tried to keep her in when the weather was rough,<br>Didn’t like to see her lean over the rail.<br>He was in a rare fright, once or twice.<br>But you know, it is just my opinion, Sir,<br>That his Lordship is rather psychic, as they say. |
| CHARLES | Were they always together? |
| DOWNING | Always, Sir.<br><br><br>That was just my complaint against my Lady.<br>It’s my opinion that man and wife<br>Shouldn’t see too much of each other, Sir.<br>Quite the contrary of the usual opinion,<br>I dare say. She wouldn’t leave him alone.<br>And there’s my complaint against these ocean liners<br>With all their swimming baths and gymnasiums<br>There’s not even a place where a man can go<br>For a quiet smoke, where the women can’t follow him.<br>She wouldn’t leave him out of her sight. |
| CHARLES | During that evening, did you see him? |
| DOWNING | Oh yes, Sir, I’m sure I saw him.<br><br><br>I don’t mean to say that he had any orders —<br>His Lordship is always most considerate<br>About keeping me up. But when I say I saw him,<br>I mean that I saw him accidental.<br>You see, Sir, I was down in the Tourist,<br>And I took a bit of air before I went to bed,<br>And you could see the corner of the upper deck.<br>And I remember, there I saw his Lordship<br>Leaning over the rail, looking at the [[motifs/water\|water]] —<br>There wasn’t a [[motifs/moon\|moon]], but I was sure it was him.<br>While I took my turn about, for near half an hour<br>He stayed there alone, looking over the rail.<br>Her Ladyship must have been all right then,<br>Mustn’t she, Sir? or else he’d have known it. |
| CHARLES | Oh yes … quite so. Thank you, Downing,<br><br><br>I don’t think we need you any more. |
| GERALD | Oh, Downing,<br><br><br>Is there anything wrong with his Lordship’s car? |
| DOWNING | Oh no, Sir, she’s in good running order:<br><br><br>I see to that. |
| GERALD | I only wondered<br><br><br>Why you’ve been busy about it tonight. |
| DOWNING | Nothing wrong, Sir:<br><br><br>Only I like to have her always ready.<br>Would there be anything more, Sir? |
| GERALD | Thank you, Downing;<br><br><br>Nothing more. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exit DOWNING] |
| VIOLET | [[motifs/well\|Well]], Charles, I must say, with your investigations,<br><br><br>You seem to have left matters much as they were —<br>Except for having brought Downing into it:<br>Of which I disapprove. |
| CHARLES | Of which you disapprove.<br><br><br>But I believe that an unconscious accomplice is desirable. |
| [[characters/chorus\|CHORUS]] | Why should we stand here like guilty conspirators, waiting for some revelation<br><br><br>When the hidden shall be exposed, and the newsboy shall shout in the street?<br><br><br>When the private shall be made public, the common photographer<br>Flashlight for the picture papers: why do we huddle together<br>In a horrid amity of misfortune? why should we be implicated, brought in and brought together? |
| IVY | I do not trust Charles with his confident vulgarity, acquired from worldly associates. |
| GERALD | Ivy is only concerned for herself, and her credit among her shabby genteel acquaintance. |
| VIOLET | Gerald is certain to make some blunder, he is useless out of the army. |
| CHARLES | Violet is afraid that her status as Amy’s sister will be diminished. |
| [[characters/chorus\|CHORUS]] | We all of us make the pretension<br><br><br>To be the uncommon exception<br>To the universal bondage.<br>We like to appear in the newspapers<br>So long as we are in the right column.<br>We know about the railway accident<br>We know about the sudden thrombosis<br>And the slowly hardening artery.<br>We like to be thought [[motifs/well\|well]] of by others<br>So that we may think [[motifs/well\|well]] of ourselves.<br>And any explanation will satisfy:<br>We only ask to be reassured<br>About the noises in the cellar<br>And the [[motifs/window\|window]] that should not have been open.<br>Why do we all behave as if the door might suddenly open, the curtains be drawn,<br><br><br>The cellar make some dreadful disclosure, the roof disappear,<br>And we should cease to be sure of what is real or unreal?<br>Hold tight, hold tight, we must insist that the world is what we have always taken it to be.<br><br><br>AMY’S VOICE. Ivy! Violet! has [[characters/arthur\|Arthur]] or John come yet? |
| IVY | There is no news of [[characters/arthur\|Arthur]] or John. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Enter AMY and [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]]] |
| AMY | It is very annoying. They both promised to be here<br><br><br>In good [[motifs/time\|time]] for dinner. It is very annoying.<br>Now they can hardly arrive in [[motifs/time\|time]] to dress.<br>I do not understand what could have gone wrong<br>With both of them, coming from different directions.<br>[[motifs/well\|Well]], we must go and dress, I suppose. I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] Harry will feel better<br>After his rest upstairs. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exeunt, except [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]]] |

| Chi parla | Battuta |

| *(didascalia)* | Il salotto, dopo il tè. Un pomeriggio di fine marzo. |  
| *(didascalia)* | AMY, IVY, VIOLET, [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]], GERALD, CHARLES, MARY |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Entra DENMAN per tirare le tende] |  
| AMY | Non ancora! Suonerò io per te. C'è ancora molta luce.<br><br><br>Non ho nulla da fare se non guardare le giornate allungarsi,<br>ora che sto in casa da ottobre a giugno,<br>e la [[motifs/swallow\|rondine]] viene troppo presto e la [[motifs/spring\|primavera]] sarà finita<br>e il cuculo se ne sarà andato prima ch'io esca di nuovo.<br>O Sole, che una volta eri così caldo, o Luce che si dava per scontata<br>quando ero giovane e forte, e sole e luce non si cercavano<br>e la [[motifs/night\|notte]] non si temeva e il giorno si attendeva<br>e ci si poteva fidare degli orologi, e il domani era certo<br>e il [[motifs/time\|tempo]] non si fermava nel buio!<br>Accendi le luci. Ma lascia le tende aperte.<br>Ravviva il [[motifs/fire\|fuoco]]. Non verrà mai la [[motifs/spring\|primavera]]? Ho freddo. |  
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | Wishwood è sempre stato un luogo freddo, Amy. |  
| IVY | Ho sempre detto ad Amy che dovrebbe andare al sud d'[[motifs/winter\|inverno]].<br><br><br>Se fossi nella posizione di Amy, andrei al sud d'[[motifs/winter\|inverno]].<br>Seguirei il sole, non aspetterei che il sole venisse qui.<br>Andrei al sud d'[[motifs/winter\|inverno]], se potessi permettermelo,<br>invece di gelare, come faccio, a Bayswater, davanti a un [[motifs/fire\|fuoco]] a gas contando scellini. |  
| VIOLET | Andare al sud! alle biblioteche circolanti inglesi,<br><br><br>alle vedove di militari e ai cappellani inglesi,<br>alla gelida sedia a sdraio e al tè forte e freddo —<br>il forte, freddo, ristagnato, cattivo tè indiano. |  
| CHARLES | Non è affatto lo stile di Amy. Siamo gente cresciuta in campagna.<br><br><br>Amy è troppo a lungo avvezza ai nostri modi,<br>vivendo fra cavalli e cani e fucili,<br>per volere mai lasciare l'Inghilterra d'[[motifs/winter\|inverno]].<br>Ma uno scapolo come me sta meglio a [[settings/london\|Londra]]:<br>un uomo può star comodissimo al suo circolo<br>perfino in un [[motifs/winter\|inverno]] inglese. |  
| GERALD | [[motifs/well\|Ebbene]], quanto a me,<br><br><br>tornerei volentieri a fare il sottotenente<br>pur d'essere di nuovo in Oriente. Un clima incomparabile<br>per chi sappia usare un po' di comune prudenza;<br>e i servitori ti curano molto meglio. |  
| AMY | I miei servitori sono perfettamente competenti, Gerald.<br><br><br>A questo bado ancora io. |  
| VIOLET | [[motifs/well\|Ebbene]], quanto a me,<br><br><br>non andrei mai al sud, no, decisamente mai,<br>anche se potessi farlo [[motifs/well\|bene]] come Amy:<br>l'Inghilterra è già abbastanza brutta, non andrei mai al sud<br>solo per vedere la gente più volgare —<br>a [[motifs/home\|casa]] propria si può star fuori dai loro piedi;<br>gente con denari venuti chissà da dove — |  
| GERALD | Dividendi di azioni aeronautiche. |  
| VIOLET | Fanno il bagno tutto il giorno e [[motifs/dance\|ballano]] tutta la [[motifs/night\|notte]]<br><br><br>col minimo assoluto di vestiti. |  
| CHARLES | È il bere cocktail che fa il male:<br><br><br>non c'è nulla al mondo di così cattivo per i giovani.<br>Tutto ciò che occorre a una persona civile<br>è un bicchiere o due di sherry secco prima di pranzo.<br>I giovani moderni non sanno che cosa bevono,<br>i giovani moderni non badano a che cosa mangiano;<br>hanno perduto il senso del gusto e dell'olfatto<br>per via dei loro cocktail e delle loro sigarette. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Entra DENMAN con sherry e whisky. CHARLES prende sherry e GERALD whisky.] |  
| CHARLES | Ecco a che si riduce. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Accende una sigaretta] |  
| IVY | La generazione più giovane<br><br><br>è indubbiamente decadente. |  
| CHARLES | La generazione più giovane<br><br><br>non è quel che eravamo noi. Non ha la tempra,<br>non ha il senso della responsabilità. |  
| GERALD | Sei molto duro con la generazione più giovane.<br><br><br>Io non li frequento più molto, per conto mio;<br>ma devo dire che ho incontrato esemplari molto per bene<br>e certi tiratori di prim'ordine — meglio di quanto fossi tu,<br>Charles, per quel che ricordo. E poi bisogna far la tara:<br>non abbiamo lasciato loro un mondo così comodo in cui vivere.<br>Lasciamo che la generazione più giovane parli da sé:<br>è la generazione di Mary. Lei che ne pensa? |  
| MARY | Davvero, cugino Gerald, se vuoi notizie<br><br><br>sulla generazione più giovane, devi chiederle a qualcun altro.<br>Temo di non meritare il complimento:<br>io non appartengo ad alcuna generazione. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Esce] |  
| VIOLET | Davvero, Gerald, devo dire che sei molto privo di tatto,<br><br><br>e credo che Charles avrebbe potuto essere più riguardoso. |  
| GERALD | Mi dispiace molto: ma perché s'è offesa?<br><br><br>Volevo solo tirarla nella conversazione. |  
| CHARLES | È una brava ragazza; ma per lei è un'età difficile.<br><br><br>Suppongo che vada per i trent'anni?<br>Dovrebbe sposarsi, ecco che cos'è. |  
| AMY | E si sarebbe sposata, se le cose fossero andate come volevo io.<br><br><br>Il ritorno di Harry non le rende le cose facili<br>in questo momento: ma la vita può ancora aggiustarsi.<br>Intanto lasciamo l'argomento. Meno se ne parla, meglio è. |  
| GERALD | A proposito, Amy,<br><br><br>quando devono arrivare tutti i ragazzi? |  
| AMY | Non voglio che l'[[motifs/clock\|orologio]] si fermi nel buio.<br><br><br>Se volete sapere perché non lascio mai Wishwood,<br>la ragione è questa. Tengo in vita Wishwood<br>per tenere in vita la famiglia, per tenerli uniti,<br>per tenere in vita me, e vivo per tenere in vita loro.<br>Nessuno di voi capisce quanto è vecchio,<br>e la [[motifs/death\|morte]] verrà a voi come una mite sorpresa,<br>un brivido d'un attimo in una stanza vuota.<br>Solo [[characters/agatha\|Agatha]] pare scoprire nella [[motifs/death\|morte]] un qualche senso<br>che io non trovo.<br>— Sono certa soltanto di [[characters/arthur\|Arthur]] e di John,<br>[[characters/arthur\|Arthur]] a [[settings/london\|Londra]], John nel Leicestershire:<br><br><br>dovrebbero essere qui tutti e due in buon [[motifs/time\|tempo]] per il pranzo.<br>Harry mi ha telefonato da Marsiglia,<br>sarebbe venuto in aereo a [[settings/paris\|Parigi]], e di là a [[settings/london\|Londra]],<br>e sperava d'arrivare nel corso della serata. |  
| VIOLET | Harry è sempre stato il più probabile a far tardi. |  
| AMY | Questa [[motifs/time\|volta]] non sarà colpa sua.<br><br><br>Siamo molto fortunati d'avere Harry, comunque. |  
| IVY | E quando prenderai la tua torta di compleanno, Amy,<br><br><br>e aprirai i tuoi regali? |  
| AMY | Dopo il pranzo:<br><br><br>è il [[motifs/time\|momento]] migliore. |  
| IVY | È la prima [[motifs/time\|volta]]<br><br><br>che non prendi la torta e i regali all'ora del tè. |  
| AMY | Questa è un'occasione molto particolare,<br><br><br>come dovresti sapere. Sarà la prima [[motifs/time\|volta]]<br>in otto anni che siamo tutti insieme. |  
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | Sarà piuttosto penoso per Harry,<br><br><br>dopo otto anni e tutto ciò che è accaduto,<br>tornare a Wishwood. |  
| GERALD | Perché penoso? |  
| VIOLET | Gerald! sai che cosa intende [[characters/agatha\|Agatha]]. |  
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | Intendo penoso, perché tutto è irrevocabile,<br><br><br>perché il passato è irrimediabile,<br>perché il futuro si può costruire soltanto<br>sul passato reale. Vagando ai tropici<br>o contro la scena dipinta del Mediterraneo,<br>Harry deve avere spesso ricordato Wishwood —<br>il tè in camera dei bambini, le vacanze di scuola,<br>le audaci imprese sul vecchio pony,<br>e avrà pensato di rientrare furtivo per la porticina.<br>Troverà un nuovo Wishwood. Adattarsi è difficile. |  
| AMY | Nulla è cambiato, [[characters/agatha\|Agatha]], a Wishwood.<br><br><br>Tutto è tenuto com'era quando lui partì,<br>tranne il vecchio pony, e il setter bastardo<br>che ho dovuto far sopprimere.<br>Nulla è stato cambiato. Ci ho badato io. |  
| [[characters/agatha\|AGATHA]] | Sì. Intendo che a Wishwood troverà un altro Harry.<br><br><br>L'uomo che torna dovrà incontrare<br>il ragazzo che partì.
